0 participants
David (Arm) ↳ replying to Rik Riel Inline Review Reviewed-by Needs Work LLM
Reviewer David (Arm) pointed out that the patch's check for a NULL pages pointer is unnecessary, as it was omitted from the original code and only added in this change. Reviewer noted that GUP-fast is inconsistent, undocumented, and error-prone, specifically questioning whether it should also flush the dcache for consistency or if this must be called with the PT lock held. Reviewer suggested breaking out a helper function for filling arrays in gup_fast_pmd_leaf and other similar functions, to avoid manual looping, and proposed distinguishing between gup and gup-fast using the VMA argument.
Show original comment 
That's not what I meant. I know that we have GUP users without
pages array.

In the code change you are performing the check is not required.

That's why there wasn't one in the first place.

---

It's all very inconsistent. And undocumented. And error prone.

Could we have GUP-fast also flush the dcache for consistency or
must that only be called with the PT lock held?

View on lore ↗
Signals: requested changes, inconsistency, lack of documentation
Rik Riel (author) ↳ replying to David (Arm) Neutral LLM
Author suggests simplifying the code by moving a check to a single location instead of relying on callers to implement it. The author is addressing David's concern that some callers of gup_fast need a dcache flush to prevent cache aliasing hazards. The author claims that only a small number of slow GUP callers require this, and suggests moving the cache flushes out of follow_page_mask() and into __get_user_pages(). Author agrees that multiple GUP callers could use a shared helper function for dcache flushing, and plans to restructure in v2
Show original comment 
It looks like 2 of the 3 callers of gup_fill_pages()
check for pages existing. The other does not.

Should we just have the check in this one place, and
not depend on the caller?

---

I think we could, but it does not look like any of
them need it.

Out of the 83 callers of gup_fast, many DMA into the
page, and the drivers do their own flushing. Also,
flushing before the DMA write doesn't help.

KVM, Infiniband, and others pin the pages, sometimes
for longer periods of time, and either flush around 
their reads and writes, or run only on architectures
where we don't need to.

The others access the page through the user's own
virtual address, which again does not require a
dcache flush.

Only a small number of slow GUP callers need dcache
flushes.

It may make more sense to move the cache flushes
all the way out into __get_user_pages() and away
from the follow_page_mask() call chain, so we can
maybe merge more of the helper functions between
slow GUP and fast GUP.

---

I think all of those could use the same helper
function I am breaking out here, once the dcache
flushing moves to its own helper.

--

Ongoing Submitted 2026-11-01 Last comment 2026-11-03 ⏱ No recent activity [PATCH] io_uring/sqpoll: do the return-to-userspace work in the SQPOLL thread Neutral LLM

The patch addresses a long-standing issue where the SQPOLL thread fails to return to userspace, causing memory.high reclaim and block cgroup throttling to occur inside a charge instead of at the top of the loop. This is due to a change in commit af5d68f8892f that replaced the call to io_run_task_work() with io_sq_tw(). The patch adds a call to resume_user_mode_work() when TIF_NOTIFY_RESUME is set, ensuring that these tasks run outside of the charge.

Ongoing Submitted 2026-10-27 Last comment 2026-11-02 ⏱ No recent activity [PATCH] kernfs: avoid READ_ONCE() on a kgid_t in kernfs_new_node() Neutral LLM

The patch addresses a sparse warning on SMP alpha by reading the gid_t inside the kgid_t in kernfs_new_node() and wrapping it back up with KGIDT_INIT(). This change is safe as it does not alter the object code before or after GCC 12 for alpha and GCC 14 for x86-64.

2 reviewer comments — 0 participants
0 participants
Tejun Heo Acked-by Positive LLM
Acknowledged the patch without any objections or concerns.
Show original comment 
On Tue, Oct 27, 2026 at 07:32:47AM -0700, Shakeel Butt wrote:
> kernfs_new_node() reads the parent's gid with READ_ONCE(attrs->ia_gid).
> That gid is a kgid_t, which is a struct.
> 
> On SMP alpha, READ_ONCE() ends by casting the value it loaded back to
> the type of its argument. Here that is a cast from a struct to the same
> struct. GCC accepts that as an extension, but ISO C does not allow a
> cast to or from a struct type, so sparse warns:
> 
>   fs/kernfs/dir.c:751:31: sparse: sparse: cast to non-scalar
>   fs/kernfs/dir.c:751:31: sparse: sparse: cast from non-scalar
> 
> Read the gid_t inside the kgid_t instead, and wrap it back up with
> KGIDT_INIT(). This is still a single marked load of the same four bytes.
> 
> There is no functional change. With GCC 12 for alpha (the config from
> the report) and GCC 14 for x86-64 (with and without KCSAN),
> fs/kernfs/dir.o has the same object code before and after this change.
>

Ongoing Submitted 2026-10-30 Last comment 2026-11-02 ⏱ No recent activity [PATCH v2 0/5] memcg: clean up memory.high enforcement 5 patches
Show 5 individual patches
  • [1/5] memcg: move memory.high enforcement out of try_charge_memcg() Neutral Submitted 2026-10-30Last comment 2026-10-31 Muchun Song
  • [2/5] memcg: force charges from reclaim before trying the limits Neutral Submitted 2026-10-30Last comment 2026-11-02 Muchun Song Michal Hocko Shakeel Butt (author)
  • [3/5] memcg: use high_work for kernel threads Neutral Submitted 2026-10-30Last comment 2026-10-31 Muchun Song
  • [4/5] memcg: use high_work for remote charges Neutral Submitted 2026-10-30Last comment 2026-10-31 Muchun Song
  • [5/5] memcg: simplify high limit handling after a charge Submitted 2026-10-30
Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch series cleans up memory.high enforcement by using a worker for kernel threads and remote charges, improving performance and handling edge cases such as reclaiming and sleeping during charging. It also forces charges from PF_MEMALLOC tasks before checking limits to prevent self-reclaiming and ensures that local user charges are handled correctly. The changes aim to provide a best-effort solution while maintaining the hard limit of memory.max for interrupts and swap. The patches modify various functions, including memcg_enforce_high(), to implement these improvements.

Last comment 2026-11-04 [PATCH] crash: keep crashkernel CMA pages reserved if activation fails Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch modifies the crashkernel memory reservation mechanism to keep CMA pages reserved even if their activation fails. This ensures that kernel data written to these pages is not overwritten by the crash kernel and remains available in the dump. The change adds a new function call, cma_reserve_pages_on_error(), which marks the areas as reserved when activation fails.

2 reviewer comments — 0 participants
0 participants
Bradley Morgan ↳ replying to Usama Arif Needs Work LLM
The reviewer noted that the patch's behavior of marking crashkernel CMA areas as reserved on activation error would also occur under every bitmap_zalloc() failure, and asked if this is accounted for.
Show original comment 
You do know that this will activate under every bitmap_zalloc() failure,
right? Is that accounted for?
View on lore ↗
Signals: requested change, uncertainty
Usama Arif (author) ↳ replying to Bradley Morgan Positive LLM
The author addressed Bradley Morgan's concern about the crashkernel CMA pages being released when activation fails, explaining that keeping them reserved ensures the first kernel doesn't overwrite or lose data from the vmcore. The author confirmed this is the intended behavior and mentioned an alternative approach in fadump.
Show original comment 
Yes, that's intended. By the time cma_activate_area() runs, the range is
already in crashk_cma_ranges[], so the crash kernel will use it no matter
why activation failed. Keeping the pages reserved is the only way to make
sure the first kernel has nothing there that gets overwritten or goes
missing from the vmcore; the area then behaves like a regular
crashkernel= reservation of the same size.

fadump made the same choice; it is what cma_reserve_pages_on_error() was
added for in commit 27d121d0ec6d ("mm/cma: provide option to opt out
from exposing pages on activation failure").

The bitmap is one bit per page (32 KiB per GiB with 4K pages) and is
allocated at core_initcall, so a failure there is very unlikely.

The alternative is to give the memory back and to drop the
range from crashk_cma_ranges[] when activation fails, leaving the crash
kernel with less memory than requested. I do think that is acceptable as
well. But we do need either whats done in this patch or give the memory
back. Happy to send the alternative as v2 is everyone thinks that the
better way forward.
View on lore ↗
Signals: acknowledged, confirmed
Last comment 2026-11-04 [PATCH] mm/hugetlb: allow crashkernel to reuse hugetlb CMA memory Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch allows the crashkernel to reuse existing hugetlb CMA memory, enabling systems that already reserve large amounts of memory with hugetlb_cma= to use some of it for the capture kernel's working set after a crash. The patch introduces a new suffix 'hugetlb_cma' for the crashkernel parameter, which allows specifying an additional amount of memory to be reused from existing HugeTLB allocations. This approach ensures that the running kernel can still access the full 6G of hugetlb CMA memory during normal operation, while providing the capture kernel with additional working memory after a crash.

2 reviewer comments — 0 participants
0 participants
Kiryl Shutsemau ↳ replying to Usama Arif Reviewed-by Needs Work LLM
Reviewer noted that the new suffix in crashkernel=size,hugetlb_cma does not provide any additional information beyond what is already stated in the documentation for crashkernel=size,cma. They suggested reusing existing hugetlb CMA memory first and reserving a dedicated area only for what hugetlb CMA does not cover.
Show original comment 
The crashkernel=size,cma documentation already says that pages allocated
from the range are not in the vmcore and that it should not be used if
dumping of userspace memory is intended. HugeTLB pages are userspace
memory. I don't see what a new suffix makes explicit that ",cma" does
not already.

Why not let crashkernel=size,cma take hugetlb CMA first and reserve a
dedicated area only for what hugetlb CMA does not cover? With
hugetlb_cma=6G crashkernel=2G,cma there would be no extra CMA at all.
With hugetlb_cma=2G crashkernel=4G,cma the crash kernel would reuse the
2G and a dedicated 2G area would be reserved for the rest. Either way,
nobody has to learn a new option.

The catch is ordering. reserve_crashkernel_cma() runs from setup_arch(),
but hugetlb_cma_reserve() runs later, from mm_core_init_early(). The arch
code could record the size and leave the reservation to generic code
right after hugetlb_cma_reserve(). That is still memblock time, so the
remainder can be declared there. An early_initcall is too late for that.

-- 
  Kiryl Shutsemau / Kirill A. Shutemov

[PATCH v2] mm/hugetlb: allow crashkernel to reuse hugetlb CMA memory Neutral llm-chunked-no-replies

This patch allows the crashkernel to reuse existing HugeTLB CMA memory instead of reserving additional dedicated CMA areas. This is particularly useful on systems that already reserve large amounts of memory with hugetlb_cma=, as it prevents the total CMA reservation from exceeding available physical memory. The patch modifies the kernel's behavior when handling crashkernel requests with the ,cma option, ensuring that the existing HugeTLB CMA pool remains available for normal operation and can be reused by the capture kernel after a crash. The patch also includes changes to various architecture-specific code to implement this new behavior.

Neutral LLM → Heuristic

Prepare for keeping a PMD-mapped anonymous THP mapped by a PMD across the swap round-trip, which makes PG_anon_exclusive have to survive in a swap PMD and not just in a swap PTE.

A LoongArch swap PMD is the swap PTE value plus _PAGE_HUGE, and _PAGE_SWP_EXCLUSIVE sits outside both the type and the offset field, so the PMD helpers can use the same bit.

10 reviewer comments — 0 participants
0 participants
Usama Arif (author) Neutral Heuristic
When reclaim swaps out a PMD-mapped anonymous THP today, the PMD is split into HPAGE_PMD_NR PTE-level swap entries via TTU_SPLIT_HUGE_PMD before unmap. This series introduces a PMD-level swap entry so the huge mapping can survive the swap round-trip and do_huge_pmd_swap_page() can restore the PMD mapping directly on swap-in, without waiting for khugepaged to collapse the range later. The PMD swap entry is a compact page-table encoding for HPAGE_PMD_NR consecutive swap slots. swap_map accounting remains per-slot and is unchanged. A PMD swap entry does not promise that the swap cache always contains one PMD-sized folio. While the cache is empty or contains one PMD-sized folio, PMD-level handling can proceed.
Show original comment 
When reclaim swaps out a PMD-mapped anonymous THP today, the PMD is
split into HPAGE_PMD_NR PTE-level swap entries via TTU_SPLIT_HUGE_PMD
before unmap. This series introduces a PMD-level swap entry so the
huge mapping can survive the swap round-trip and do_huge_pmd_swap_page()
can restore the PMD mapping directly on swap-in, without waiting for
khugepaged to collapse the range later.

The PMD swap entry is a compact page-table encoding for HPAGE_PMD_NR
consecutive swap slots. swap_map accounting remains per-slot and is
unchanged. A PMD swap entry does not promise that the swap cache always
contains one PMD-sized folio. While the cache is empty or contains one
PMD-sized folio, PMD-level handling can proceed. Once the cache has
split/per-slot state, users either inspect the individual slots directly
(mincore) or split the PMD swap entry and retry through the PTE path
(fault, swapoff, MADV_WILLNEED, UFFDIO_MOVE). MADV_FREE does not consult
the cache: it frees a whole PMD swap entry in place and only splits when
the advised range covers part of the PMD. Likewise, if any slot is still
backed by zswap's per-page store, PMD-order swap-in consumers split and
let the PTE path load the range page by page; an all-on-disk range can
still be read back as one PMD-sized folio.

The series is ordered so every consumer can handle PMD swap entries
before the swap-out producer starts installing them. The swap-out patch
is the last functional change.

Performance (measurements from v8):

  Measured with vm-scalability's case-swap-w-seq benchmark [1]. Four
  pinned workers repeatedly write a 6 GiB anonymous working set on a
  4 vCPU / 4 GiB guest, forcing the set out to swap and back in. Swap
  is an 8 GiB NOCOW raw virtio device (cache=none, aio=native), zswap is
  disabled, and THP enabled/defrag are both "always". The numbers below
  are medians of five interleaved runs per kernel after one warm-up run:

  Metric                          Baseline     Patched      Change
  Aggregate throughput            584.3 MiB/s  2408.9 MiB/s +312.2% (4.12x)
  Elapsed time                    85.43 s      20.72 s      -75.7%
  Major faults                    1,814,699    228,466      -87.4%
  Swap I/O rate                   1.02 GiB/s   4.10 GiB/s   +303.9%

  This swap-intensive synthetic workload measures the reduction in
  swap-fault and page-table overhead from preserving PMD mappings.
  For long-running workloads, I expect benefits from fewer TLB misses,
  less khugepaged work and retaining larger folios. I have not yet found
  a benchmark that captures those benefits well. PMD swap entries also
  move us closer to eliminating page-table deposits for anonymous THPs,
  which would save memory.

Intermediate patches and Sashiko:

  Earlier patches prepare consumers while reclaim still requests
  TTU_SPLIT_HUGE_PMD. PMD swap entries cannot reach those consumers until
  the swap-out producer is enabled. Concerns requiring live PMD swap
  entries before that patch describe an unreachable state. See [2].
  The v8 Sashiko report failed to apply and supplied no findings.

Notes on zswap:

  Native PMD-order zswap load/store is left for a follow-up.
  Alexandre Ghiti is currently working on this.
  This series can still preserve PMD swap entries while zswap is enabled:
  zswap stores the THP as order-0 entries, and PMD-order swap-in
  consumers split any range that has zswap entries before reading it.
  If zswap has written the whole range back to disk, or the swap cache
  still contains one PMD-sized folio, PMD-level handling can proceed.

[1] https://git.kernel.org/pub/scm/linux/kernel/git/wfg/vm-scalability.git/tree/case-swap-w-seq
[2] https://lore.kernel.org/all/282ef982-3e48-4283-9155-73a33fc1c4e8@linux.dev/

v8 -> v9: https://lore.kernel.org/all/20261002095503.3585565-1-usama.arif@linux.dev/
Patch numbers below refer to v9.
- Rebase onto akpm/mm-new at dba8aba0a9a0.

  and splitting the zswap preparatory changes. (Zi Yan, Yosry Ahmed)
- Patches 3 (loongarch) and 6 (s390): order the PMD exclusive helpers as
  test/mk/clear, matching their adjacent PTE helpers. (Huacai Chen)
- Patch 7: describe the exclusive-bit assertion in terms of not aliasing
  _PAGE_PSE, without making the constraint specific to 32-bit. (Zi Yan)
- Patch 8: add pmd_swp_clear_flags() alongside the PTE helper and use it
  in softleaf_from_pmd(). Keep consumers before the swap-out producer:
  no PMD swap entry can be installed in the intermediate patches. (Zi Yan)
- Patch 9: check that setting swap soft-dirty leaves the PMD non-present.
  (Lance Yang)
- Patch 10 (new): extract the reference fix already present in v8 from
  the migration-splitting API cleanup, now patch 11. A concurrent
  MADV_DONTNEED can zap or partially split the migration PMD and cause
  the old freeze=true path to strand the extra folio reference. Remove
  that reference and use freeze=false with the old API. Drop patch 11's
  claim that the PMD must still be a migration entry. (Zi Yan)
- Patch 14: explain that -EIO requests a GFP_KERNEL allocation retry,
  while other errors fail the fork. (Zi Yan)
- Patches 15-16: keep shared swap-cache allocation error cleanup and
  high-order zswap rejection separate. The new base already exports
  zswap_is_present(), so drop the separate locking documentation patch.
  (Yosry Ahmed)
- Patch 17: rename the helper to folio_has_any_hwpoisoned_page(), add a
  high level description and update all three callers, including the
  PTE-batching and PMD-fault paths in patches 29 and 30. (Zi Yan)
- Patch 28: clear the source UFFD marker before destination RWP re-arming,
  matching move_swap_pte(). (Lance Yang)
- Patch 30: leave a cached folio that is not PMD-sized in the swap cache
  on PTE fallback; it may still be a zswap writeback folio under I/O.
  (Lance Yang)

v7 -> v8: https://lore.kernel.org/all/20260914122950.3283997-1-usama.arif@linux.dev/
- Cover letter: add the case-swap-w-seq performance results. (Andrew Morton)

  PMD helpers and express them the same way, as pte_pmd()/pmd_pte()
  wrappers over the PTE helpers. (David Hildenbrand)
- Patch 6 (s390): drop the comment above the helpers, keep the explanation
  in the RSTE swap layout above __SWP_OFFSET_MASK_RSTE, and move the
  helpers into the existing CONFIG_ARCH_HAS_PMD_SOFTLEAVES block above
  pmd_swp_soft_dirty(). (David Hildenbrand)
- Patch 7 (x86): add static_assert(_PAGE_SWP_EXCLUSIVE != _PAGE_PSE) so a
  32-bit build that ever selects ARCH_HAS_PMD_SOFTLEAVES fails to compile
  rather than producing pmd_present() swap entries. (Kiryl Shutsemau)
- Patch 10: rename the flag to to_migration_entries, use two-tab
  continuation indentation, reword the split comment, turn the comment on

  pmd_trans_huge() || pmd_is_valid_softleaf() test, which was both too loose
  and silently skipped the split, in favour of a VM_WARN_ON_ONCE() at the top
  of __split_huge_pmd_locked() asserting that to_migration_entries implies a
  present or device-private PMD. (David Hildenbrand)
- Patch 11: drop the VM_WARN_ON_ONCE()/force and its comment from the swap
  decode arm - 10/29 now asserts the to_migration_entries contract at the top
  of __split_huge_pmd_locked() instead - and build the replacement PTEs by
  advancing pte_next_swp_offset() rather than rebuilding each entry, which
  hoists the soft-dirty/uffd/exclusive tests out of the loop. (David
  Hildenbrand).
- Patch 12 (new): split the swap-side changes out of the fork patch, so the
  swap-entry range duplication gets its own patch for the swap maintainers.
  Keeps the single-slot names as inline wrappers so existing callers are
  untouched. (David Hildenbrand)
- Patch 13 (fork): report a failed swap dup as -EIO and let copy_pmd_range()
  own the GFP_KERNEL retry, as the PTE path does, dropping the open-coded
  retry loop; move the mm counter update into each entry-type arm. (David
  Hildenbrand)
- Patch 16 (smaps): smaps_account_swap() takes nr_pages rather than a byte
  size, and the local is called swapcount. (David Hildenbrand)
- No other functional change.  All twelve sashiko findings on v7 were
  analysed and none are defects of this series.

v6 -> v7: https://lore.kernel.org/all/20260818131202.494754-1-usama.arif@linux.dev/
- Rebase onto akpm/mm-new at baa8de2f3448 and adapt to the new

- Patches 2-9: split the six architecture helpers from generic detection and
  add debug_vm_pgtable coverage.  Use the s390 RSTE exclusive bit, make the
  x86 helper return bool, clear all PMD swap overlay bits before softleaf
  decoding, and add the architecture maintainers to Cc.

  migration-splitting API and keep the PMD swap split separate.
- Patch 12: reject a multi-slot duplication range that crosses a swap-cluster
  boundary.
- Patch 13: drop the zswap_load() change, now upstream as 1a904e0d3c43, and
  check for zswap after swap-cache insertion while all slots are pinned.
- Patch 14: scan every subpage for hardware poison, discard a failed or newly
  allocated unmapped PMD-sized folio before PTE fallback, and recheck the PMD
  before splitting it.
- Patches 15-23: split the non-present PMD walkers by subsystem.  Add the
  guard-advice patch so MADV_GUARD_INSTALL/REMOVE leaves PMD swap entries
  whole; the other split patches preserve v6 behaviour.
- Patch 24: honour the current THP policy, discard a failed PMD-sized folio,
  and recheck the PMD before PTE fallback.
- Patch 25: do not split after cached-folio revalidation loses a race; retry
  so a restored present THP remains whole.
- Patches 26-27: separate the independent PTE-batching hardware-poison fix,
  scan subpages directly, and discard failed or never-mapped PMD-sized folios
  before PTE retry instead of making the whole range fail with SIGBUS.
- Patch 28: keep the normal swap-out path unchanged, but make failed producer
  preconditions warn and return -EBUSY rather than BUG or return -EINVAL.
- Patch 29: grow the selftests from 16 to 17, adding
  swapin_sync/cache-residency coverage and more robust feature, fallback,
  privilege, data-integrity, and swap-device-priority handling.

v5 -> v6: https://lore.kernel.org/all/20260722152043.2273289-1-usama.arif@linux.dev/
- Add patch 1 to rename pmd_to_softleaf_folio() to

  softleaf rather than silently returning NULL. (Dev Jain)
- Patch 4: bound the fork extend-table fallback to one retry, re-read
  the PMD under its lock, normalize unrecoverable copy_huge_pmd() errors
  to -ENOMEM so copy_pmd_range() cannot clear and leak the source swap
  PMD, and drop a redundant thp_migration_supported() gate.
- Patch 5: check multi-page swap-cache insertions for zswap-backed slots
  in __swap_cache_add_check() under the cluster lock, both before
  allocation and before insertion, and reject mixed zswap/disk state
  with -EBUSY. (Yosry Ahmed, Nhat Pham)
- Patch 6: on a failed non-uptodate PMD-order read, remove the large
  folio from swap cache before splitting so order-0
  fallback retries individual slots rather than poisoning the whole
  2 MiB range; retain hardware-poisoned folios for per-subpage handling.
- Patch 7: make HMM snapshot mode report a PMD swap entry as non-resident,
  matching PTE swap entries, rather than HMM_PFN_ERROR.  Drop redundant
  thp_migration_supported() gates and simplify non-present PMD handling.
- Patch 8: factor PMD MADV_WILLNEED prefetch into
  swapin_pmd_swap_entry(), split and retry through PTEs after any
  PMD-order swapin failure, and replace the racy folio_test_locked()
  plus folio_lock() sequence with folio_trylock().
- Patch 9: guard PMD-swap UFFDIO_MOVE code with CONFIG_THP_SWAP, clarify
  RWP marker propagation, and reject a PMD swap entry at the destination
  with -EEXIST so UFFDIO_MOVE cannot loop forever on -EAGAIN.
- Patch 10: honor current THP/VMA policy before PMD-order swap-in, recheck
  that the PMD is still the original swap entry before splitting for PTE
  fallback, and provide the CONFIG_TRANSPARENT_HUGEPAGE wp_huge_pmd()
  declaration/stub needed by THP=n builds.
- Patch 11: make an invalid set_pmd_swap_entry() walk context warn and
  return -EINVAL instead of falsely reporting success and corrupting the
  MM_ANONPAGES/MM_SWAPENTS accounting, and add an exact PMD-size folio
  precondition check. (Luiz Capitulino)
- Patch 12: use /proc/swaps for prerequisite detection, check
  MADV_HUGEPAGE, and distinguish an environment that cannot allocate a
  PMD THP (SKIP) from a swap-out validation failure (FAIL).  Add
  partial-mprotect and partial-munmap split coverage.  Strengthen
  munmap/MADV_FREE VmSwap accounting, pagemap slot-offset checks, and
  mprotect/mremap swapped-state checks; force mremap to move, check
  munmap()'s return, and mark the UFFDIO_MOVE destination MADV_HUGEPAGE
  before asserting PMD restoration.  Move common setup and cleanup into
  one fixture, merge the swapoff fixture, remove the redundant cycles
  test, and make the data pattern differ between base pages so the split
  tests can detect incorrect slot ordering.  Order fork-COW so the parent
  writes while the child still holds the untouched shared swap entry.
  (Luiz Capitulino)
- Clarify commit messages throughout.  Retain TTU_SPLIT_HUGE_PMD after
  prototyping its removal: removing it here requires an extra rmap walk
  and broadens the series beyond PMD swap entries. (Matthew Wilcox)
- Rebase onto akpm/mm-new from 15 August (4b65683fd25f).

v2 -> v3: https://lore.kernel.org/all/20260602142537.198755-1-usama.arif@linux.dev/
- Clarified the PMD swap entry rule: it is a compact encoding for
  HPAGE_PMD_NR swap slots, not a guarantee that swap cache always has
  one PMD-sized folio. (Lance Yang)
- Swapoff, fault, MADV_WILLNEED, and UFFDIO_MOVE now classify the
  whole PMD swap-cache range and split/retry through the PTE path for
  split/per-slot cache state. (Lance Yang)
- mincore handles PMD swap entries without assuming one lookup covers
  a split swap-cache range. (Lance Yang)
- UFFDIO_MOVE rechecks all HPAGE_PMD_NR slots before moving an empty
  PMD swap-cache range, avoiding stale rmap metadata for per-slot
  cached folios.
- Added a standalone zswap prerequisite patch from Alexandre that
  distinguishes all-on-disk large-folio ranges from ranges with
  per-page zswap entries.
- Replaced the global zswap-ever-enabled policy with per-range zswap
  checks: PMD swap entries can still be installed while zswap is
  enabled, and PMD-order swap-in consumers split when the range has
  per-page zswap state.
- Added a mincore selftest and updated MADV_WILLNEED coverage so the
  test checks that the PMD swap entry remains in place until first
  touch.  Total pmd_swap coverage is now 14 tests.

2.53.0-Meta
View on lore ↗
Usama Arif (author) Acked-by Reviewed-by Neutral Heuristic
pmd_to_softleaf_folio() reads as if it converted a PMD into a folio. What it does is decode the softleaf entry stored in the PMD and return the folio that entry references - the direction softleaf_to_folio() already spells out. Prepare for keeping a PMD-mapped anonymous THP mapped by a PMD across the swap round-trip, which makes PG_anon_exclusive have to survive in a swap PMD and not just in a swap PTE. s390 is the one architecture where a swap PMD is not a swap PTE in disguise: it is an RSTE with its own layout, converted to a fake PTE swap entry for the common code. Give it its own exclusive bit rather than borrowing the PTE-format macro, and document it in the RSTE swap layout, where bit 52 was listed as unused. --- arch/s390/include/asm/pgtable.h | 20 ++++++++++++++++++-- 1 file changed, 18 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-) diff --git a/arch/s390/include/asm/pgtable.h b/arch/s390/include/asm/pgtable.h index 2d5c2ab06de98..2c6b5205ee3bc 100644 --- a/arch/s390/include/asm/pgtable.

swap round-trip, which makes PG_anon_exclusive have to survive in a swap
PMD and not just in a swap PTE.

s390 is the one architecture where a swap PMD is not a swap PTE in
disguise: it is an RSTE with its own layout, converted to a fake PTE swap

2.53.0-Meta
View on lore ↗
Usama Arif (author) Acked-by Reviewed-by Neutral Heuristic
Reclaim splits a PMD-mapped anonymous THP into PTE-level swap entries before unmapping it, so an ordinary swap entry has never had to appear in a PMD. Prepare for installing one there instead: the softleaf layer is where every consumer decodes non-present PMDs. Accept swap entries as valid PMD softleaves and add pmd_is_swap_entry(). A swap entry carries no PFN, so make pmd_softleaf_to_folio() warn and return NULL rather than interpret a swap offset as a page frame number. Unlike migration and device-private entries, a PMD swap entry can also carry the swap-exclusive marker, which softleaf_from_pmd() has to strip before decoding. Strip all three overlays unconditionally while we are here: each clear is a plain bit clear, so testing first only buys a branch. --- include/linux/leafops.
Show original comment 
Reclaim splits a PMD-mapped anonymous THP into PTE-level swap entries
before unmapping it, so an ordinary swap entry has never had to appear in a
PMD. Prepare for installing one there instead: the softleaf layer is where
every consumer decodes non-present PMDs.

Accept swap entries as valid PMD softleaves and add pmd_is_swap_entry().
A swap entry carries no PFN, so make pmd_softleaf_to_folio() warn and
return NULL rather than interpret a swap offset as a page frame number.

Unlike migration and device-private entries, a PMD swap entry can also
carry the swap-exclusive marker, which softleaf_from_pmd() has to strip
before decoding. Strip all three overlays unconditionally while we are
here: each clear is a plain bit clear, so testing first only buys a branch.

+#ifdef CONFIG_ARCH_HAS_PMD_SOFTLEAVES
+static void __init pmd_swap_exclusive_tests(struct pgtable_debug_args *args)
+{
+	swp_entry_t entry;
+	softleaf_t softleaf;
+	pmd_t pmd;
+
+	if (!has_transparent_hugepage())
+		return;
+
+	pr_debug("Validating PMD swap exclusive\n");
+	entry = args->swp_entry;
+
+	pmd = softleaf_to_pmd(entry);
+	softleaf = softleaf_from_pmd(pmd);
+
+	WARN_ON(pmd_swp_exclusive(pmd));
+	WARN_ON(!softleaf_is_swap(softleaf));
+	WARN_ON(memcmp(&entry, &softleaf, sizeof(entry)));
+
+	pmd = pmd_swp_mkexclusive(pmd);
+	softleaf = softleaf_from_pmd(pmd);
+
+	WARN_ON(!pmd_swp_exclusive(pmd));
+	WARN_ON(!softleaf_is_swap(softleaf));
+	WARN_ON(pmd_swp_soft_dirty(pmd));
+	WARN_ON(memcmp(&entry, &softleaf, sizeof(entry)));
+
+	pmd = pmd_swp_clear_exclusive(pmd);
+	softleaf = softleaf_from_pmd(pmd);
+
+	WARN_ON(pmd_swp_exclusive(pmd));
+	WARN_ON(!softleaf_is_swap(softleaf));
+	WARN_ON(memcmp(&entry, &softleaf, sizeof(entry)));
+}
+#else  /* !CONFIG_ARCH_HAS_PMD_SOFTLEAVES */
+static void __init pmd_swap_exclusive_tests(struct pgtable_debug_args *args) { }
+#endif /* CONFIG_ARCH_HAS_PMD_SOFTLEAVES */
+
 static void __init pte_swap_tests(struct pgtable_debug_args *args)
 {
 	swp_entry_t arch_entry;
@@ -1322,6 +1363,7 @@ static int __init debug_vm_pgtable(void)
 	pmd_leaf_soft_dirty_tests(&args);
 
 	pte_swap_exclusive_tests(&args);
+	pmd_swap_exclusive_tests(&args);
 
 	pte_swap_tests(&args);
 	pmd_softleaf_tests(&args);

Usama Arif (author) Reviewed-by Neutral Heuristic
Prepare for keeping a PMD-mapped anonymous THP mapped by a PMD across the swap round-trip, which makes PG_anon_exclusive have to survive in a swap PMD and not just in a swap PTE. riscv encodes a swap PMD exactly like a swap PTE, so the new helpers wrap the PTE ones and reuse _PAGE_SWP_EXCLUSIVE. --- arch/riscv/include/asm/pgtable.h | 15 +++++++++++++++ 1 file changed, 15 insertions(+) diff --git a/arch/riscv/include/asm/pgtable.h b/arch/riscv/include/asm/pgtable.h index 2ebd5fa5adc58..fda94dba9bb3e 100644 --- a/arch/riscv/include/asm/pgtable.

 #ifdef CONFIG_ARCH_HAS_PMD_SOFTLEAVES
+static inline bool pmd_swp_exclusive(pmd_t pmd)
+{
+	return pte_swp_exclusive(pmd_pte(pmd));
+}
+
+static inline pmd_t pmd_swp_mkexclusive(pmd_t pmd)
+{
+	return pte_pmd(pte_swp_mkexclusive(pmd_pte(pmd)));
+}
+

+{
+	return pte_pmd(pte_swp_clear_exclusive(pmd_pte(pmd)));
+}

Last comment 2026-11-05 [PATCH v2] x86/lib: Use REP MOVSB for large memcpy() on ERMS CPUs without FSRM Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch optimizes large memory copies on ERMS CPUs without FSRM by using the REP MOVSB instruction from 2048 bytes onwards, reducing execution time by up to 66.8%. The patch also folds memcpy_orig() into __memcpy() and makes adjustments for FSRM CPUs. Smaller copies continue to use a scalar loop for performance reasons.

1 reviewer comment — 0 participants
0 participants
David Laight ↳ replying to Usama Arif Inline Review Reviewed-by Needs Work LLM
Reviewer David Laight noted that the patch does not handle misaligned copies of size 512 to 640 bytes correctly, which were slower than the scalar loop in v1, and requested raising the threshold from 512 to 2048 bytes. Reviewer David Laight questioned the patch's assumption that REP MOVSB would always improve performance on ERMS CPUs, citing specific alignment issues on Zen and Intel CPUs, including a case where REP MOVSB is slower due to misalignment between source and destination buffers.
Show original comment 
On Wed,  4 Nov 2026 14:28:06 -0800
Usama Arif <usama.arif@linux.dev> wrote:

Which cpu type was that?
Do I remember the older zen cpu falling back to byte copies?
I wonder if it is possibly to do a run-time patch of the constant.
In all the tests I've done on Intel cpu (going back to sandy bridge)
the only alignment that made any difference was 64 byte (32 on older cpu)
alignment of the destination buffer.

That could be 'for further study'.

There are some very bad alignment pairs on some cpu.
On zen5 if (IIRC) (src - dest) % PAGE_SIZE is between 1 and 127 then
the both setup and per-byte cost of REP MOSVB are significantly higher
(the byte alignment makes minimal difference throughout).
I don't remember finding anything similar on the Intel cpu I tested.
But it is probably not worth checking for them because the cost of the
comparison will be larger overall.

>Yes, that's intended. By the time cma_activate_area() runs, the range is

>from exposing pages on activation failure").

>The bitmap is one bit per page (32 KiB per GiB with 4K pages) and is
>allocated at core_initcall, so a failure there is very unlikely.
>
>The alternative is to give the memory back and to drop the
>range from crashk_cma_ranges[] when activation fails, leaving the crash
>kernel with less memory than requested. I do think that is acceptable as
>well. But we do need either whats done in this patch or give the memory
>back. Happy to send the alternative as v2 is everyone thinks that the
>better way forward.

Last comment 2026-11-03 Re: [PATCH v2 1/2] mm: zswap: use separate compression and decompression requests Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch separates compression and decompression requests in zswap, allowing multiple tasks to access different pages concurrently without blocking on each other's operations. The existing shared request mechanism caused a task to block until the previous task released its mutex, but this patch introduces separate requests that eliminate this dependency. This change does not require any additional worker threads or CPU migrations and is designed to improve concurrency in zswap operations.

1 reviewer comment — 0 participants
0 participants
Kanchana Sridhar ↳ replying to Usama Arif Reviewed-by Needs Work LLM
The reviewer expressed concern that the patch could cause compression contexts to starve under memory pressure, potentially leading to deadlocks with page-faults in extreme scenarios.

Re: [PATCH v2 1/2] mm: zswap: use separate compression and decompression requests Neutral llm-chunked-no-replies
Last comment 2026-11-03 Re: [PATCH] x86/lib: Use REP MOVSB for large memcpy() on ERMS CPUs without FSRM Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch optimizes large memory copies on ERMS CPUs without FSRM by using the REP MOVSB instruction, which is more efficient than scalar code. The patch introduces a new threshold of 2048 bytes for using REP MOVSB and keeps scalar code for smaller copies. It also modifies the existing 32-byte check to use REP MOVSB and RET on FSRM CPUs, removing the need for a non-FSRM entry jump. Additionally, it aligns replacement storage before its start label to ensure that the raw FSRM return retains its thunk before relocation. The patch results in significant performance improvements, with a 66.8% reduction in copy time for 4 KiB copies on Cooper Lake CPUs.

1 reviewer comment — 0 participants
0 participants
David Laight ↳ replying to Usama Arif Inline Review Reviewed-by Needs Work LLM
Reviewer David Laight noted that the ITS (Instruction Translation Service) patching mechanism may not work correctly when using REP MOVSB for large memcpy() on ERMS CPUs without FSRM, as returns are patched before alternatives are copied. Reviewer David Laight expressed uncertainty about whether the cache line alignment of the code affects performance and requested clarification. Reviewer David Laight suggested using %rdx instead of %rcx for the 'rep movsb' instruction, preserving %rcx for the return, and recommended swapping cx<=>dx in the rest of the function to accommodate this change.

Last comment 2026-11-03 Re: [PATCH] x86/lib: Use REP MOVSB for large memcpy() on ERMS CPUs without FSRM Neutral LLM (per-reviewer)

This patch modifies the x86 library to use REP MOVSB instructions for large memcpy() operations on ERMS CPUs that lack FSRM support. The change addresses a potential issue where a RET instruction in the lower half of a cache line could cause problems. To mitigate this, the replacement code is modified to swap the RDX and RCX registers, which makes it easier to review and maintain.

2 reviewer comments — 0 participants
0 participants
David Laight ↳ replying to Usama Arif Inline Review Needs Work LLM
Reviewer noted that the alignment of __memcpy() itself is not relevant, but rather the alignment of the replacement in .altinstr_replacement, and requested a specific change to mitigate RET instructions in the lower half of a cache line Reviewer David Laight pointed out that if __memcpy() is not aligned, the relocated RET in the replacement might be in the second half of a cache line and would require mitigation, but the current patch uses a mitigation-free ret.
Show original comment

---

So the RET in the replacement is in the first half a cache line- so becomes a ret.
But, unless __memcpy() is aligned, the relocated RET might be in the second
half so would need mitigating - but you copy in a mitigation-free ret.

If that is the only mitigation then wouldn't aligning memcpy mean you could

Usama Arif (author) ↳ replying to David Laight Needs Work LLM
Author corrected David Laight's understanding of how RET instructions are handled in the code, explaining that a RET in the first half of a cache line keeps the jump to its_return_thunk safe and does not require mitigation. Author acknowledged that the REP MOVSB replacement in .altinstr_replacement needs alignment, but decided not to align __memcpy() itself due to performance considerations on certain CPUs.
Show original comment 
It's the other way round: a RET in the first half keeps the jump to
its_return_thunk, see cpu_wants_rethunk_at():

        return !((unsigned long)addr & 0x20);

So the copy is the thunk jump, which is safe in either half.

---

It isn't the only one: with the retbleed, SRSO or call depth tracking
thunk every return needs it, so the code has to use RET and let
apply_returns() pick. For ITS alone, aligning __memcpy() would save one
direct jump on FSRM CPUs with ITS (Ice Lake to Rocket Lake), which I
don't think is worth it.
